The Batman Part II Could Combine Victor Zsasz And The Court Of Owls

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Amid the rumors surrounding The Batman: Part II, the best solution might be to merge two truly disturbing Batman villains. Matt Reeves' The Batman established a particularly grim vision of DC's Gotham City, where corruption extends into almost every institution. Robert Pattinson's Bruce Wayne is still relatively early in his career as the Dark Knight, but his investigation into the Riddler's murders has already exposed just how deeply criminal activity is embedded in the city's foundations. The sequel has an opportunity to take that world in an even more disturbing direction.

With production underway and several new cast members joining Pattinson in The Batman: Part II, speculation about the story has intensified. Sebastian Stan, Charles Dance and Scarlett Johansson are among the additions, although their precise character assignments have not all been officially confirmed – despite what some outlets have reported. Meanwhile, leaked set photographs, production details and fan theories have prompted considerable discussion about the kind of antagonists Reeves might introduce.

One particularly interesting possibility would bring together two very different threats from Batman's comic-book history. Rather than giving the Dark Knight another serial killer to investigate or another powerful organization to expose, The Batman: Part II could connect the two, creating a conspiracy in which Gotham's most influential citizens employ an exceptionally dangerous individual to eliminate anyone threatening their interests.

One particularly interesting The Batman: Part II theory comes from Reddit user Low_Bridge_1141, who suggested that Victor Zsasz could effectively become the Court of Owls' personal assassin. The theory proposes that Zsasz might be employed to murder members of Mayor Bella Reál's task force, preventing them from uncovering the organization's criminal activities. Rather than operating independently, he would function as a hired killer carrying out the orders of Gotham's wealthiest and most powerful citizens.

It is an intriguing possibility because Zsasz is one of Batman's most disturbing adversaries. First appearing in Batman: Shadow of the Bat #1 in 1992, Victor Zsasz is a serial killer who carves a tally mark into his own skin for every person he murders. Unlike the Riddler, who wants Batman to recognize his intellectual superiority, Zsasz has no elaborate theatrical motivations – he simply enjoys killing.

The Reddit theory draws comparisons with Birthday Boy, a serial killer employed by the Penguin in the graphic novel Batman: Earth One. The suggestion is that Reeves could adapt a similar relationship, with a powerful Gotham criminal using a particularly vicious murderer to eliminate inconvenient witnesses.

The appeal of combining these antagonists in The Batman: Part II is that they represent two different kinds of corruption. Zsasz is an individual whose violence is intensely personal, while the Court of Owls is an institutional conspiracy that has allegedly influenced Gotham for generations. The organization consists of wealthy Gothamites who secretly manipulate the city through political influence, financial power, and murder. Its traditional assassins, known as Talons, are particularly dangerous opponents.

Giving Zsasz a similar position to the Talons would allow Reeves to preserve the grounded crime-thriller atmosphere of the first movie while introducing the much larger conspiracy hinted at. Rather than immediately presenting them, The Batman: Part II could establish the Court of Owls' influence through ordinary-looking people who use money and political connections to get away with murder.

It would also create an interesting problem for Batman. In the first film, Bruce investigates individual crimes and gradually discovers the connections between Gotham's criminal underworld and its supposedly respectable leadership. If Zsasz begins murdering members of Reál's task force, Batman could initially assume that the Penguin is responsible. Only after investigating the killings might he discover that somebody considerably more powerful is orchestrating events.

The Reddit discussion even proposes that Kenzie, the corrupt GCPD officer introduced in The Batman, could become Zsasz's first victim after the Court begins worrying that he might reveal information. That would give the sequel an immediate connection to the first movie while demonstrating how far the Court is prepared to go to protect itself.

Most importantly, the idea would give Pattinson's Batman an antagonist who is frightening for reasons beyond physical strength. The Riddler exposed Gotham's corruption, but Zsasz could demonstrate what happens when the people responsible for that corruption decide to silence anyone investigating them. Pairing an unpredictable murderer with a secret society that has spent generations manipulating Gotham would make the central conspiracy in The Batman: Part II particularly unsettling.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Trouble stealing a car? There's an app for that.

Vice City's glow-up in GTA 6 ushers in a new era of smart car tech, bringing keyless ignition to the series. That's great for car owners, but a pain for our criminal duo since you can't hotwire a keyless car. Luckily, that doesn't mean you can't steal a car with high-tech security. Modern problems call for modern crimes, and that's where the key cloner comes in.

Standard trackers on stolen cars aren't much of a problem—they just forbid you from making modifications at a mod shop unless you register the car as your own.

Advanced trackers, however, are a whole other beast. They relay your exact location to the cops, but you can outrun the signal if you're fast enough. Keep your foot on the gas, and the cops won't be able to track you until you slow down. Apparently, the solution to beat high-tech surveillance is still a good ol' fashioned pedal-to-the-metal joyride.

Trump administration rules out cyberattack behind week of tech outages

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Vice President JD Vance canceled his DealBook Summit appearance to attend meetings on the outages, which hit banks, airlines and agencies

The Trump administration said on Wednesday it sees no indication of a coordinated cyberattack, state-sponsored activity or any other malign actor behind the recent tech outages.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton and Secretary of State Marco Rubio, who also serves as national security adviser, described the incidents as separate events. They said companies patching vulnerabilities and upgrading outdated systems caused them, according to the White House.

"This is not a cyberattack, and this is not a coordinated effort by a nation state," Clayton said. Rubio said officials have "no indication, no evidence, and no reason to believe at all that any of these incidents are related to each other." He said the Microsoft $MSFT incident has nothing to do with the FAA incident, and the FAA incident has nothing to do with the banking problems of a few days ago.

Clayton said institutions are taking systems down to install patches after vulnerabilities were identified. He said some handoffs between systems are not coming back as expected. "This is just an upgrade in patching, an upgrade in systems," he said. Rubio said, "We are not under systemic attack."

Vice President JD Vance backed out of his slot at The New York Times DealBook Summit on Wednesday so he could sit in on meetings about the outages, according to CNBC.

CNBC said a senior administration official spoke with reporters during a phone call and asked not to be named. The official said the government has "a great deal of confidence" that the incidents stem from system upgrades, patching and hardware changes, or other "disparate" events. Asked how much artificial intelligence played a role, the official said it is "challenging to answer that question across all of these incidents," according to CNBC.

The disruptions in recent days range from a nationwide ground stop and a prolonged Outlook failure at Microsoft to a reported blackout on the classified communications network at the National Security Agency, according to CNBC. The outlet also listed software troubles at JPMorgan $JPM Chase, flight delays tied to a United Airlines outage, and glitches with Apple $AAPL products.

Clayton told CNBC on Tuesday that no cyberattack lay behind the outages. He offered the House Homeland Security Committee the same message on Wednesday, according to CNBC.

Sen. Mark Warner, a Virginia Democrat and the vice chairman of the Senate Intelligence Committee, told CNBC he wants “better coordination” on AI safety. “I understand why people are freaked out,” Warner said.

The Federal Aviation Administration attributed last week's nationwide ground stop to legacy equipment that became unstable when data flooded its network. The agency said its AI-powered SMART system was not connected to the disruption.

Microsoft said it had no indication that malicious activity caused its global disruption of Microsoft Outlook, Teams and Copilot. The company said adjustments it rolled out to boost serviceability were "not taking as expected."

GTA 6's Subscription-based Perks are Worrying Players

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The subscription offers an exclusive vehicle, garage, discounts, and other perks in Leonida

Grand Theft Auto 6 has finally arrived to critical acclaim. Millions of players purchased their copies a while ago, and preorders came with a free month of GTA Plus, Rockstar's monthly subscription service. It now offers exclusive benefits in GTA 6's single-player campaign, including a new car, a garage, discounts, and other gameplay bonuses.

Previously focused primarily on GTA Online, GTA Plus is now expanding into Rockstar's latest open-world adventure. The subscription costs $7.99 per month in the United States, and its GTA 6 benefits are only available while players maintain an active membership.

Here's everything reportedly included with GTA Plus in GTA 6:

These benefits are tied to an active GTA Plus membership. Canceling the subscription means losing access to the exclusive car and garage, along with the other membership perks. Players who would rather experience GTA 6 without the subscription bonuses can also disable them. Simply open the pause menu, head to the Account tab, find GTA Plus Boosts, and toggle them off.

Not everyone is happy about Rockstar bringing its subscription service into the single-player experience. A Reddit post highlighting the new GTA Plus benefits has attracted thousands of upvotes, with many players criticizing the decision to lock additional content behind a monthly fee.

Some of the most popular comments take issue with the exclusive car and garage, arguing that these features should be included in the base game rather than requiring another subscription. Others have pointed out that even players who purchased more expensive editions of GTA 6 still have to maintain GTA Plus to access the benefits.

Others worry that introducing subscription-exclusive content into the single-player campaign could signal what's coming when GTA Online eventually arrives in GTA 6. While the current benefits are largely optional, the decision has already prompted questions about how much additional content Rockstar might eventually lock behind GTA Plus.

Researchers review isotope methods for tracing and quantifying gaseous nitrogen production in soils

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Researchers from the Institute of Applied Ecology (IAE) of the Chinese Academy of Sciences have reviewed stable nitrogen isotope techniques for tracing the sources and quantifying the production of gaseous nitrogen in soils. The review examines four approaches for distinguishing microbial pathways that produce nitrous oxide (N₂O), nitric oxide (NO) and dinitrogen (N₂).

Stem cell–derived nanoparticles modulate immune cells and help delay type 1 diabetes in mice

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

In people with type 1 diabetes, the immune system mistakenly identifies insulin-producing cells in the pancreas as foreign and attacks them, leaving the body unable to properly regulate blood sugar. While insulin therapy helps manage the disease, it does not stop the underlying immune attack.

Microsoft Rolls Out Its Biggest Bidding Upgrade In A Decade via @sejournal, @brookeosmundson

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Webinar: 9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Microsoft Advertising is rolling out rebuilt automated bidding models. Learn what is changing for Target CPA and Target ROAS campaigns and what to review now.

Microsoft Advertising is rolling out what it calls its biggest bidding model upgrade in a decade. The new models use more signals to predict whether an ad click will turn into a conversion and what it might be worth.

Microsoft will apply the upgrade automatically as it reaches more accounts. That means campaigns can start spending differently before you make any changes yourself. Microsoft announced the rollout on November 18 and plans more bidding updates in 2027.

Microsoft says the new models can change how quickly campaigns spend and how closely they stick to your targets. These are the areas to keep an eye on:

A $50 CPA target still leaves room for a campaign to bring in conversions at $40 each, even if it spends its full budget. A max CPC can limit that flexibility. Say you average $3 a click and cap bids at $5. The cap rules out a $6 bid even if Microsoft expects the click could bring in a conversion within your CPA target.

Say you raise your CPA target on Monday and customers usually take a week to buy. By Friday, you know what Monday’s clicks cost, but you may still be waiting on some of the sales. Faster bidding won’t shorten the time those customers take to buy.

Raising a CPA target or lowering a ROAS target can open up auctions your campaign previously passed up. To limit swings in performance, Microsoft suggests making those changes in 15% increments and reviewing each after one conversion cycle. For a bigger change, like switching bid strategies or adding a conversion goal, Microsoft recommends allowing one to two cycles.

Microsoft recommends conversion-based bidding for new campaigns once the account has at least five conversions. Those can come from campaigns you already run. If you’re still gathering your first five, Enhanced CPC is an option.

On LinkedIn, Sophie Westall asked if the same advice applies to low-volume accounts already using Enhanced CPC. Microsoft Advertising Liaison Navah Hopkins replied:

Every account is different so a blanket recommendation still needs to be put to the test. With that said, so long as the account has at least 5 conversions total, the system is smart enough to use that to begin with conversion-based bidding. While we still recommend the 30-day evaluation period and not making too many changes if you’re working with fewer conversions, the system is much more responsive now!

For judging performance, Microsoft recommends around 30 conversions in 30 days at the campaign or portfolio level. A low-volume campaign can reach the end of that first month and still have too few conversions to judge the switch.

Microsoft reported 10% more conversion value per click at roughly the same ROAS after the model upgrade. That result came from August 2026 global Search and Audience Network data for advertisers using Maximize Conversion Value or Target ROAS.

Microsoft also looked at advertisers who switched bid strategies. Search advertisers who moved from Target CPA to Target ROAS saw 8% more conversion value at a similar ROAS. Advertisers who moved from Enhanced CPC to Maximize Conversions with a CPA target saw 18% more conversions at a similar CPA.

If you’re considering Target ROAS, pull up a few recent purchases in Microsoft Advertising and compare the reported values with the order totals. A $40 order and a $400 order can both show up as conversions even if your tracking assigns them the same value. That’s easy to miss if you’re used to checking that purchases are coming through and leaving it there.

Microsoft reported its largest gains from value-based bidding among advertisers who sent actual transaction values. If your purchase goal uses a fixed value, the reported ROAS reflects that estimate. It can move as you sell more or spend less, but it won’t capture a change in how much customers spend per order.

With lead gen, you may need time to get a better read on what a conversion is worth. Two people can submit the same form, and sales may find out a week later that only one is a fit.

Pedro Bailaderia asked Navah Hopkins about sending an estimate at submission and updating it once more information comes into the CRM. Hopkins supported using the Conversions API or offline imports. She also advised setting a ROAS target that reflects the values you send.

If your team is still working out how to value a qualified lead, that’s worth settling before you set a ROAS target. A campaign can hit the target you set while sales is still waiting for more leads it can work with.

If a campaign already brings in the sales or leads you need at a cost you’re happy with, you can give the upgrade some room before changing anything. Keeping your settings steady makes it easier to see how the new models work with the goals and budget you already have.

The rollout may be more useful if you’ve been adjusting targets to get a campaign to spend more evenly. If delivery settles down on its own, you may have fewer reasons to touch those targets during the week. That would leave you freer to change them when the business needs more volume or can afford to pay more for a conversion.

GTA 6 New Game Plus: Can You Continue Playing After The Ending?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 has a pretty vast world to explore, and you shouldn't be in any rush to clear the story. However, if you opt to continue the campaign at a brisk pace, you can pick up some incredibly useful tools, many of which make it easier to steal cars or get through the hurdles you'll encounter on your adventure.

It's a constant push and pull about how long you should wait before starting a story mission and moving on to a new part of the game. Here's what happens after you decide to finish GTA 6, how it handles a potential New Game+, and how to wrap everything up for a 100% completion rating and a coveted Platinum trophy.

There are no direct spoilers for the events of the ending of GTA 6, but be warned, we do mention the logistics of the endgame.

Again, we won't spoil the ending of GTA 6 here; we'll just explain what happens after the ending and what you can expect going forward if you decide to finish it.

Essentially, the game "reverts" to a point before you started the final mission of the game. This is a major decision and pretty obvious, as Jason and Lucia wait outside a law office, with the option to go inside and kick off the finale.

If you never set food inside, you can keep playing the game as normal and go for a 100% rating with Jason and Lucia. If you finish the game, you'll warp back to this point with all the progress you have so far.

In other words, the choice to finish the story is entirely up to you. Keep an extra save file handy in case you change your mind, as the finale takes a bit of time.

Yes, you can keep playing past the ending, and there's a small twist with how the game continues to track your progress. Previously, when saving, the game gave you a completion percentage based on how much story progress you made. So near the end, you're hitting a 96% or 98% completion rating.

Once you finish the game and head back to the pre-finale, the game will track your total clear percentage. Since the story is completed, you don't need to track that progress anymore, so everything is converted into a comprehensive tracking system. Using the progress menu, you can track exactly what you need to reach 100%.

To get a full 100% rating, you need to do a lot of legwork, like scanning individual cars and observing animals, as well as clearing out rival gang operations and fencing a certain amount of illegal substances, jewelry, and marked bills.

There is no traditional New Game Plus mode that we can find active at launch in GTA 6. It seems like for now, you can't replay the entire story from the beginning with all your upgrades and items. But at the very least, you can still explore the entire major Vice City area and get your 100% rating.

No matter if you’re new to the world of audiobooks or a seasoned listener, Amazon is making it super easy (and extremely tempting) to take the plunge on an Audible subscription this Black Friday—or, you know, literally right now!

As an added bonus, Amazon is also offering a $20 credit, which pretty much means you can get an additional audiobook or two for free. Now that’s what I call a Christmas miracle!

In case you can’t decide which audiobook to listen to first, I’ve rounded up some of the season’s buzziest titles—all available with your shiny new membership. You’re welcome!

A Court of Splintered Harmony by Sarah J. Maas

Sunrise on the Reaping by Suzanne Collins

Not familiar with Audible? That’s where my role as your literary fairy godmother comes in. Audible, an Amazon-owned brand, is home to millions of audiobooks, including titles that are exclusive to the platform (Think Project Hail Mary and Dungeon Crawler Carl, which I’ve been seeing ALLLL over BookTok!). Trust me when I tell you that audiobooks are the perfect companion for traveling to visit family or hectic holiday shopping trips—and with this Audible deal, your next three months of listens are handled!

Because I know how busy life gets during the holidays, I read the fine print so you don’t have to. Once your three months are up, your membership will auto-renew for $8.99 a month—honestly worth it considering, as I’m 99.9% positive audiobooks are about to become your new obsession. Just note that this deal is only valid for new Audible members—so if you currently have a membership (or have taken advantage of an Audible promotional deal more than twice in the past 14 months), you’re sadly not eligible. One November storm changed how California’s Sierra Nevada looked from orbit

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

An early storm brought nearly a foot of snow to Donner Pass, offering a first look at the snowpack that helps supply California with water.

Fresh snow stretches across California’s Sierra Nevada in a satellite image taken on the morning of November 14.

Around Lake Tahoe and farther south, white covers much of the high country.

Along part of the range’s western side, green slopes still show through, making it easy to see where the storm left its mark.

A Pacific storm swept through the previous day, bringing heavy rain to the Sacramento area and close to a foot of snow to some mountain locations.

A break between weather systems gave NASA’s Terra satellite a clear look at the mountains after one of the season’s first major snowfalls.

The instrument that captured the view, the Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer, or MODIS, takes in long stretches of the mountain range at once.

From that vantage point, the snow that fell on individual peaks and mountain passes comes into view as a broad band of white.

NASA Earth Observatory paired the November 14 view with an image from two days earlier, when snow was largely confined to the highest ridges southeast of Lake Tahoe.

The earlier view gives a sense of how much changed in the time it took for one storm to cross California.

A separate regional analysis from the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration (NOAA) estimated that snow covered about 43 percent of its Sierra Nevada analysis area on November 14.

A month earlier, the estimate was less than 1 percent. The comparison shows how quickly snow can spread across terrain that was largely bare in mid-October.

At Donner Pass, northwest of Lake Tahoe, the Central Sierra Snow Laboratory recorded nearly 12 inches of snow on November 13.

Officials closed Interstate 80 in both directions over Donner Summit that evening.

Farther south, Mammoth Mountain reported 9 to 13 inches of fresh snow. In Yosemite National Park, officials closed several roads ahead of the storm.

The reports from Donner Pass and Mammoth give a sense of how much snow fell at specific locations across the long stretch of mountains visible from above.

The storm also brought heavy rain to the Sacramento area, west of the Sierra Nevada.

Elevation helped determine what reached the ground. Where the air was warm enough, precipitation fell as rain.

Higher in the mountains, colder conditions allowed snow to accumulate.

The green slopes along the western side of the range are a reminder that a storm can bring very different weather to places only a short distance apart.

The broad cover visible on November 14 raises a question that matters well beyond the mountains.

How much water is in all that snow? Hydrologists answer it by measuring snow water equivalent, the depth of water that would remain if the snow at a given spot melted.

A foot of light, powdery snow can hold less water than a shallower layer of dense, wet snow.

California’s Department of Water Resources uses that measurement to help forecast how much water could flow out of the mountains as the snow melts.

Some of that runoff reaches major reservoirs in spring and early summer, helping supply water during the state’s dry months.

Timing matters because California receives much of its precipitation during the cooler part of the year.

Rain can reach streams and reservoirs soon after a storm.

Snow can stay in the mountains for months before melting, releasing some of that water later, when storms are less frequent.

The November storm arrived about six weeks into the water year, the 12-month period hydrologists use to track precipitation and runoff beginning October 1.

The Sierra Nevada snowpack generally peaks around April 1, leaving several months for new storms to add to it or for warm spells to melt some of what has already accumulated.

More snow was already in the forecast when NASA released the image on November 18.

The National Weather Service had issued a winter storm warning for parts of the Sierra Nevada through November 20, with around four feet of snow possible at some locations above 8,000 feet.

The November 14 image captures the mountains at the start of that longer process.

In a single day, snow spread across enough of the Sierra Nevada to change how the range looked from orbit.

Over the coming months, the same slopes will collect or lose snow with each shift in the weather.

By spring, what remains will help determine how much meltwater reaches California’s reservoirs as the state heads into its dry season.

Image Credit: NASA Earth Observatory/Michala Garrison, using MODIS data from NASA EOSDIS LANCE and GIBS/Worldview

Check us out on EarthSnap, a free app brought to you by Eric Ralls and Earth.com.

Our favorite accessories to level up your gaming setup ahead of GTA VI

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for a headset or a pro-grade controller for your return to Vice City? We have some recs.

Looking for a headset or a pro-grade controller for your return to Vice City? We have some recs.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If you buy something from a link, The Verge may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

With Grand Theft Auto VI set to finally launch tomorrow, November 19th, our long-awaited return to Vice City is nearly here. But after all this time, some of the gear you’ve used in San Andreas might feel out of place on the glamorous streets of Leonidas County. Luckily for you, we’ve gathered a few of our favorite Xbox and PlayStation 5 accessories to help your long gaming sessions run as smoothly as possible.

The 8BitDo Ultimate 3E — an upgraded pick for Xbox Series X and Series S consoles — is a very capable, comfortable controller with elite-level features, including a high polling rate and adjustable triggers. It’s on the expensive side at $149.99, but the customization and clean execution justify the cost. Read our review.

PlayStation 5 owners looking for a controller upgrade, meanwhile, might consider the DualSense Edge, Sony’s first-party pro-style controller. In addition to a variety of extra function buttons and paddles, the wireless gamepad sports adjustable trigger heights and swappable joysticks (in case the potentiometers start to drift). It’s a little bit heavier than Sony’s regular DualSense, and the battery life isn’t quite as good, but it offers customization you won’t find on the console’s default controller. Read our review.

Sony’s pro-style controller, the DualSense Edge, might suffer from shorter battery life than the standard DualSense model, but it features great software integration with the PlayStation 5 and plenty of customizable components.

Nothing’s worse than having to pause a heist so you can go hunt down a fresh set of AA batteries. The PowerA Duo Charging Station includes a rechargeable battery and replacement rear doors for two Xbox Series X / S or Xbox One controllers. Simply set them on the dock, and they’ll start charging.

PowerA’s PlayStation equivalent charges two DualSense Wireless Controllers simultaneously, with an included AC adapter, and comes with a two-year warranty.

The 2026 Roku Pro Series is the brand’s first OLED, and the 55-inch version retails for $999.99, which is on the low end of the price spectrum for OLED. Games feel richer and more immersive on OLED panels, thanks to their deeper black levels, which are the result of unlit pixels or sections of the screen. Each Pro Series panel also sports a 120Hz refresh rate, four HDMI 2.1 ports, and both low latency and variable refresh rates for gaming.

For a more premium offering, the Sony Bravia 8 II is one of our favorite screens for movies, with an engaging, immersive picture and excellent image processing. It has great gaming features, too, like a 120Hz refresh rate with variable refresh and an automatic low-latency mode. It’s not cheap, but the OLED makes for an excellent upgrade to your setup. Read our review.

Sony’s latest first-party headset, the $99 PlayStation Pulse Wireless, launches the same day as GTA VI. My colleague Cameron Faulkner recently went hands-on with the wireless headset ahead of its launch on November 19th, and came away impressed with its overall audio quality and comfort. The newest Pulse is also straightforward to set up and use, especially when paired with devices like the PlayStation Portal that support PlayStation Link, Sony’s proprietary low-latency audio connection. It also offers Bluetooth for use with other devices.

For Xbox gamers, it’s hard to beat the SteelSeries Arctis Nova 3X, which retails in multiple colors for $109.99 or less. The wireless headset is lightweight, comfortable, and compatible with a wide variety of other consoles and mobile devices as well, for those rare moments when you aren’t playing GTA VI.

If you’d rather the simplicity of a wired option, Sony’s InZone H6 Air skips the batteries and dongles, instead opting to plug straight into your controller. This open-back gaming headset is lightweight and comfortable, which is great for long hours and late nights. My colleague Cameron Faulkner described its sound as “spectacular,” with several equalizer options available for helping fine-tune the sound to your liking. Read our review.

Ikea’s Poäng chair balances affordability and comfort, with wide, supportive armrests that are just the right height for holding a controller. It comes in a variety of frame materials and cushion colors, too, making it easy to match your other furniture or console. This is my personal chair for console gaming, and I recently added a matching ottoman to the set for maximum relaxation.

Grimes & Elon Musk Feud Over AI ‘Sex Slave,’ Singer Urges Ex to ‘Step Up’ and Be ‘the King He Used to Be’

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"A man who worries so publicly abt the birth rate should not publicly advocate for its demise," she wrote.

Grimes and Elon Musk are just like any pair of exes with lingering disagreements. They just so happen to be hashing them out on a social media platform that one of them owns, and the root of their conflict just so happens to be about controversial technology that the musician’s former partner has plans to invest billions in.

In a series of back-and-forth posts on X (which Musk has owned since 2022) on Wednesday (Nov. 18), Grimes and the billionaire tech entrepreneur — who share three children — butted heads over Claudia, a new “influencer” fully generated by artificial intelligence. After Musk reposted a video of Claudia with the endorsement, “Super intelligence will hack your limbic system,” the Canadian artist directly replied to him, writing, “The fixation on fake women feels like cope.”

“I’m a fan of Claudia, but … she isn’t a she, shes an alien creature who’s both blessed and haunted by the entirety of ancestral memory,” Grimes continued. “Secualizing a species that cannot have sex is the ultimate definition of Nietzsche’s last man, and we can do better than reducing ‘it’ to a sex slave.”

Musk replied to this with “Take the White Pill,” alongside which he also shared a video of Claudia “singing” a song about how AI is destined to help humans live better lives.

But Grimes doesn’t seem like she’s taking the “white pill” any time soon. To one commenter who’d pushed back on her initial comment by asserting that “Most men don’t read Nietzsche,” the electro-pop musician tagged Musk and wrote: “Our leaders should read Nietzsche and Elon has read Nietzsche … He’s an unelected king. He could and should step into the role and confront his responsibility to society, if he wanted to.”

She went on to say that her ex “can do better” and should “rise above it,” adding, “He shud be the king we all wish he was. The king he used to be. Ima stop saying this in private and say it online. If he wud step up and be magnanimous he could earn the Mandate of Heaven – which currently he has lost.”

Musk jumped in on this thread as well, clapping back with, “I don’t want to be king and Starship has the Mandate of Heaven,” referencing his reusable SpaceX spacecraft.

In more replies to other commenters on the topic, Grimes also wrote of Musk: “a man who worries so publicly abt the birth rate should not publicly advocate for its demise” as well as “If Elon respected women in public the whole world cud change.”

Though the former couple clearly have some differences in opinion, both of them are among AI’s biggest champions in general. It was recently announced that Grimes will star in the first-ever AI film coming to theaters, lending her real voice to a character whose appearance and movements were all artificially generated.

She also plans to explore the complexities of the tech in her upcoming Psy Opera album, which she said is split into two parts. “I really didn’t want any of this [first part] to be about Elon. Not at all,” she said on Proto Pop in October. “It’s about being even more traumatized by the late-stage empire than I am about other things.”

Where Italy stand in FIFA World Rankings after latest international fixtures

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Italy will end the calendar year in 14th place in the FIFA World Rankings following a 3-1 victory at home against France and a 4-1 defeat away against Belgium in the Nations League during the November international break.

Italy began the November international break rated as the 15th best national team in the world.

The 3-1 victory at home against France temporarily saw the Azzurri rise up to 13th, but the latest update to the FIFA Men’s World Rankings, which arrived on Thursday, sees them drop back down to 14th following the completion of the latest round of international fixtures.

There have been no changes to the top four-ranked national teams since the start of the November international break: World Cup winners Spain remain top of the rankings despite their recent defeat against England, World Cup finalists Argentina remain in second, France are still in third despite their defeat against Italy and England remain in fourth.

Morocco have risen two places in the rankings up to fifth, Portugal have dropped down one place to sixth, Carlo Ancelotti’s Brazil have also dropped one place down to seventh, Belgium remain in eighth place, the Netherlands remain in ninth and Colombia have risen one place to complete the top 10.

Currently ahead of Italy in the rankings are Germany in 11th (up two places), Switzerland in 12th and Mexico in 13th (down three places).

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

As overdose deaths fall, White House makes misleading claims about who deserves credit

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Families look on as President Donald Trump speaks during a ceremony to sign the "Halt All Lethal Trafficking of Fentanyl Act" in the East Room of the White House on July 16, 2025. Evan Vucci/AP hide caption

When the White House announced big improvements in the number of people dying from fatal drug overdoses earlier this week, officials pointed to hopeful new data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention showing U.S. drug deaths have plunged to their lowest level in roughly a decade, saving nearly 50,000 lives each year.

But leading drug policy researchers say the White House statement also made misleading claims about the timing and cause of the remarkable public health success when it said that gains occurred primarily over the last 18 months due to policies enacted under President Trump's leadership.

"The historic decline in drug overdose deaths demonstrates that America is unequivocally winning the fight against illicit drugs," White House Office of National Drug Control Policy director Sara Carter said in a statement.

Carter credited heightened border security under Trump and claims of success dismantling "narco-terrorist" organizations for the recovery. "Critically, this represents a an approximately 41.5% decrease in total drug overdose deaths compared to the peak under the previous administration," the statement said.

What the White House failed to disclose is that declines in overdose fatalities began much earlier, under the Biden administration, with most of the gains occurring before Trump returned to office.

"Since the spring of 2023, we have seen a steady decline in overdose deaths," said Vanda Felbab-Brown, a drug policy expert at the Brookings Institution. "The fact that it has been sustained is very important."

According to CDC data, reductions in drug deaths began in 2023 and have continued steadily for at least 39 consecutive months — not 18 months as the ONDCP statement said. By the time Biden left office, fatal overdoses had already dropped by roughly one-third, an improvement described by addiction experts at the time as unprecedented.

Nabarun Dasgupta, a street drug researcher at the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill, was one of the first scientists to detect the stunning drop in overdose deaths. He said the trend toward fewer deaths began in some regions and among some demographic groups as early as 2021 and rapidly gained momentum nationwide.

"It was really the period early 2024 through spring 2025 where we were seeing the greatest rate of decline that we've ever seen," Dasgupta said.

Deaths from fentanyl smuggled into the U.S. from Mexico first began to rise in 2013 during President Obama's time in office and accelerated during Trump's first term. Fentanyl overdoses spiked sharply the first two years of Biden's presidency, during the COVID-19 pandemic. Despite progress, fentanyl remains the leading cause of drug deaths nationwide.

Dasgupta, Felbab-Brown and other and addiction experts credit the Biden administration for rolling out policy responses that included wider access to healthcare, making the opioid overdose medication naloxone more readily available, and negotiating a deal with China that disrupted the flow of industrial chemicals used to make fentanyl.

President Joe Biden met China's President President Xi Jinping in Woodside, Calif., in November 2023, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperative conference. China agreed to curtail shipments of the chemicals used to make fentanyl, the drug at the heart of the U.S. overdose epidemic. Doug Mills/Pool/The New York Times hide caption

The Biden team also scored significant drug interdiction wins against the Mexican cartels that smuggle fentanyl. "We have certainly been seeing over many years, going back to the Biden administration, important efforts to crack down against them," said Felbab-Brown.

Dasgupta and Felbab-Brown both said improvements in reducing drug mortality have continued under Trump. They said it's important to have an accurate timeline of the overdose crisis because researchers are still trying to understand why deaths involving fentanyl surged and why so many fewer people are now dying.

NPR asked the White House about progress in the fentanyl fight under Biden. In an emailed statement, ONDCP director Carter, a former podcaster, journalist and Fox News contributor, blamed Biden for failing to secure the U.S.-Mexico border and for allowing drug deaths to surge in the first place.

"While drug overdose deaths eventually began to decline following the peak, the Biden Administration doesn't get to take credit for overseeing the highest drug overdose numbers in history, then bringing them down from their own failure," Carter said.

Responding to CDC data showing drug deaths began to plummet much earlier than stated the White House announcement, Carter said "this could be misinterpreted as progress on a graph, but it was not progress."

According to Carter, the Trump administration is now well-positioned to build on overdose death reductions made over the last three years, in part by maintaining border security and by continuing to "relentlessly attack" drug gangs.

"We are dismantling and eliminating the violent cartels and drug trafficking organizations through coordinated operations," she said.

But Felbab-Brown at Brookings said she doesn't share the Trump administration's confidence that there has been significant progress "dismantling and eliminating" narco-terrorist organizations.

"I cannot think of one narco-terrorist organization that has been dismantled," she said. "They all continue operating and they are all very strong. They all continue smuggling drugs."

Military experts have also questioned the effectiveness, and legality, of Trump's ongoing strikes against civilian boats in the Caribbean and eastern Pacific, described as smuggling vessels by the Pentagon.

Dasgupta, the addiction researcher at UNC, said he's also concerned about whether progress reducing drug deaths can be sustained next year as the Trump administration implements deep cuts to Medicaid passed by the Republican Congress.

The Congressional Budget Office has estimated the cuts could mean as many as 10 million Americans losing access to health insurance coverage.

"Medicaid is the largest funder of addiction services in the United States," Dasgupta said. "Cutting Medicaid eligibility or making it harder to access forms of effective addiction treatment is guaranteed to leave people out in the cold."

NPR does not offer or accept money for coverage or interviews. Anyone who makes such an offer or request is not an NPR employee and does not represent NPR.

Even the PS5 Pro is "not powerful enough" to run GTA 6 at 60FPS or 40FPS, Rockstar says

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Don't expect GTA 6 to get an update supporting higher framerate modes, at least, not on current-generation consoles. Rockstar has explained that even the most powerful console of the bunch, the PS5 Pro, isn't powerful enough to run the lavish game at 40FPS or 60FPS.

Last week, some players weren't too happy with the news that GTA 6 is targeting 30FPS on all platforms it's currently launching on - PS5 Pro, PS5, Xbox Series X and Xbox Series S. Like countless other big productions this generation, the hope was that Rockstar Games would offer both quality and performance modes for its lush open-world monster.

Rockstar's now seemingly confirmed that its crime epic won't run at 60FPS or 40FPS anytime soon, simply because it can't on the currently available hardware.

"While the PS5 Pro's central processor is slightly better than the PS5 base console, it is not powerful enough to run the game's complex simulation at higher frame rates," the studio's technology production director, Marc Chambers, said in a statement to Kotaku. There you have it, then - even the beefiest console around can't handle Leonida at higher framerates.

Chambers did note some of the souped up console's other technical improvements, however: "Our base presentation makes use of ray-traced reflections and global illumination. On the PS5 Pro, this is enhanced with the addition of ray-traced shadows, as well as overall added clarity through the use of PlayStation Spectral Super Resolution (PSSR). The PS5 Pro features an upgraded graphics processor and upscaling technology that we leverage to increase the game's visual fidelity."

The tech gurus over at Digital Foundry gave some more insight into how GTA 6 runs on current-gen machines, calling it a "technical tour de force," though one that can still dip below 30FPS on PS5 and Xbox Series X during busier chase scenes and explosive shootouts. It's perhaps unsurprising, then, that higher framerates are off the table for now.

A 30FPS launch is by no means a departure for Rockstar Games, either. GTA 5 also ran at an unsteady 30FPS when it first dropped onto the Xbox 360 and PS3, with 60FPS support only coming alongside the game's PC port more than a year later. I'm not too fussed about the game's 30FPS target, personally. I'd much rather have a game that consistently hits 30FPS than one that continuously chugs trying to reach 60FPS, especially when it's as frequently drop dead gorgeous as GTA 6.

The slightly lower framerates haven't affected the game's critical response so far, either. In Eurogamer's GTA 6 review, our Chris Tapsell called it a "game of opposites and awe, minute detail and impossible scale" as "Rockstar pushes back the horizon of video games once again."

If you're planning to jump into the game at midnight - or if you're virtually travelling to New Zealand for early entry - there's a few things to be aware of. First is that you won't be able to replay GTA 6's missions without multiple save files, so be sure to soak it all up. Second, some Ultimate Edition content is only available in the first chapter.

The Turkey Trot Trap: Exercise Isn’t Currency

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 18, 2026 | Reviewed by Davia Sills

Each Thanksgiving, nearly 1.3 million Americans participate in a Turkey Trot, typically a morning run with a cute name and sometimes a cute T-shirt. With over 1,500 events organized each year, Thanksgiving is officially the biggest race day of the year. And why do people wake up early on a holiday, when school and work are closed, to run in the cold?

AI will give you plenty of reasons: family bonding, charity, or just dodging kitchen duty. But the real reason most people lace up in the freezing cold? To “earn the feast.” It sounds like an innocent joke, but clinically, treating movement as currency to pre-pay for calories is known as compensatory exercise. When workouts become a tool to cancel out guilt, research shows, it stops being healthy movement and starts predicting disordered eating.

I want to push back on the idea that we need a psychological pass for a holiday meal.

Food is many things: fuel, culture, comfort, and community. And, holistically, culture, comfort, and community can all be pleasurable. Americans have a complex relationship with pleasure; we’re always vacillating over whether pleasure is a human right or a thing to be earned.

We all know, or maybe have been, the friend who cannot (insert something fun) until they’ve zeroed their inbox, cleaned the house, or crossed off the to-do list, and somehow we never quite balance the books. Whether we forgo the fun or dilute it with guilt, we never think we’ve done enough to earn our relaxation.

This issue of feeling undeserving of pleasure comes up in so many therapy sessions. Trying to leave a bad relationship—do I deserve better? Wanting to pursue your passion—have I done enough to earn the right to follow my dream? Exhausted by a life of obligations—can I do something that is not in service of my job or my kids?

If we distill our culture into two teachers, one teacher tells us we need to earn our pleasure by working hard and being good. And since hard and good are never defined, we always feel like we haven’t yet earned the right to pursue pleasure. The other teacher tells us that life is short and to go find the joy. I’d argue that right now, that teacher is not the one hosting the Turkey Trots.

Thanksgiving. Friendsgiving. A holiday where we share one meal with loved ones we don’t see often enough. A holiday where we feel warm nostalgia as we eat the same pie we’ve had since we were kids. A holiday where we get to see elderly relatives, possibly for the last time. A holiday where we laugh in the kitchen, watch football, spend time with people we love, and yes, eat—because it’s a meal. Why do we think we need to earn any of this? We are allowed to pursue pleasure—in food, in friends—simply because we are human.

Holland LA, Brown TA, Keel PK. Defining Features of Unhealthy Exercise Associated with Disordered Eating and Eating Disorder Diagnoses. Psychol Sport Exerc. 2014 Jan 1;15(1):10.1016/j.psychsport.2013.10.005. doi: 10.1016/j.psychsport.2013.10.005. PMID: 24391457; PMCID: PMC3876288.

Stevens CJ, Baldwin AS, Bryan AD, Conner M, Rhodes RE, Williams DM. Affective Determinants of Physical Activity: A Conceptual Framework and Narrative Review. Front Psychol. 2020 Dec 1;11:568331. doi: 10.3389/fpsyg.2020.568331. PMID: 33335497; PMCID: PMC7735992.

Rockstar has made some big changes to the way GTA 6 handles, but you can switch some key buttons to go back to old controls

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Classic controls in GTA 6 are a feature that many will have wanted to see, as it's been 13 long years since the release of Grand Theft Auto 5. Many of you have been dipping in and out of that world consistently throughout that time, and if that's the case, there's a couple of aspects of the 2026 release which will come as a bit of a culture shock – particularly the way criminal profiles and GTA 6 Wanted Levels function.

Still, one of the biggest and most jarring changes may well be the new default control scheme that Rockstar has introduced. If muscle memory is getting in the way of you having a good time, you can turn on a Classic control scheme in GTA 6 in the options menu.

If you'd like to have an experience that's close to that of GTA 5, you should activate the Options/Menu button on your controller and navigate to Settings to find the option to switch from the default scheme to the Classic control scheme in GTA 6.

This will make a few key changes to the way the game handles.

For starters, the GTA 6 default control scheme has running set to L3/LS (rather than tapping X/A, which is now assigned to jumping). Melee has also been shifted to Square/X.

While you're here, you should also check that you're happy with the way the HUD communicates information. Rockstar has introduced a more dynamic system for the GTA 6 HUD – pulling non-essential information off of the screen to better showcase the scale and fidelity of the world around you.

The standard Dynamic HUD means you need to press down on the D-pad to bring up key elements such as the minimap and your bank balances – not to mention your health, stamina, and focus bars. These can all be toggled on or off in the Settings menu.

You can also alter how to view nearby tasks and locations (set at default as having you hold down on the D-pad and pressing L1/LB and R1/RB to cycle between them and set new destinations) and how to interact with the citizens of Leonida from inside of your car.

Why Did CoinShares Sell All Its Treasury Ethereum?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

CoinShares has sold all of the Ether it held in its corporate treasury, worth $16.6 million, along with half of its Solana holding, worth $2.7 million. The sales raised $19.3 million to help pay for a share buyback of up to $40 million. The company disclosed the move in its third-quarter 2026 earnings report on November 18.

The reason is a change in how the company wants to use its capital. Rather than hold digital assets on its balance sheet, CoinShares is moving that money into buying back its own stock.

Companies usually buy back shares when they believe their stock is undervalued, since fewer shares outstanding increases each remaining shareholder’s stake. By choosing buybacks over holding Ether, CoinShares is signaling that it sees better value in its own equity than in its crypto holdings.

The ETH sale also comes as Ether has fallen sharply, losing about 14% recently.

The buyback came alongside mixed quarterly results:

The higher profit on much lower revenue suggests that earnings were helped by items outside core fee income. The company has not said which ones in the figures released so far.

Ether is still testing support between about $1,730 and $1,800 on the daily chart. The level has been tested several times over the past few days, but no daily candle has closed below it.

The near-term outlook is for Ether to hold above its recent lows for at least one to two weeks, in a pause from the crash rather than a full reversal. The daily RSI is starting to recover but remains in oversold territory, which leaves room for more sideways or relief trading. Bullish momentum is still missing.

A break below current support is not expected in the next few days, but it remains a risk over the coming weeks. If it happens, the $1,650 level would come into play first, followed by the $1,510 to $1,570 zone.

For the second time since initially being added to Netflix back in 2014, Scandal is set to leave the streaming giant in December. Plus, we'll always have Olivia and Fitz. Well, we won't always have them on Netflix, because Scandal is leaving on December 17. However, if you'd like to keep watching the show, you can stream it on Hulu too.

Florence Pugh Responds to Plastic Surgery Speculation With a Reminder About Aging: ‘Faces Change’

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“The fact that people can’t even tell between what’s aging and what’s surgery is disturbing.”

After Evie magazine posted a video discussing whether Florence Pugh got plastic surgery, the actress shut down the speculation with a reminder of how aging works. “Guys. I turned 30,” she wrote. “I’m a woman. Body’s and faces change. It’s not that deep, the fact that people can’t even tell between what’s aging and what’s surgery is disturbing. Sadly puppy fluff doesn’t last forever ❤️”

Commenters also pointed out that the video featured older photos of Pugh, who has been acting steadily since her teen years. (She was just 18 when her first film, The Falling, came out in 2014.)

In her ELLE Women in Hollywood cover story, Pugh touched on aging and the comments actresses get about their appearance. It’s critical, she said, to be accepting of yourself amid all that noise.

“There’s so much scrutiny of how we look, how we dress, and how we age,” she said. “And then when we do those things, they’re like, ‘Oh my God, she looks like this, or she’s done too much of that, or she’s gained weight, or she’s lost too much weight.’ You can never get any footing right. I feel like the best work that I’ve ever done is when I truly feel ugly. When I’ve got shit makeup on and I’m either sick or ugly or having a hangover or a breakdown. I love that type of makeup because it allows me to be free.

“What I love about acting is, I don’t have the typical self-consciousness of just being a woman,” she continued. “When I’m acting, I get to be comfortable in the body that I’m in. If the camera picks up that I have a stomach roll when I’m sitting down, then I have a stomach roll when I’m sitting down. And if the camera picks up that I’ve got cellulite on my thighs, then yeah, I’ve got cellulite on my thighs. I just give it over to the art. So performing is very freeing for me, and it always has been.

“It is so crucial to be okay with how you are and who you are, because people are going to attack you regardless,” she added. “They’re going to say really shitty things, no matter if you’re too small, too big, too wrinkly, too spotty, too loud, too quiet, too opinionated. They’re going to find issue with you, because that’s just the nature of the internet and people and opinions. So it’s really important to find peace with what you want to represent and who you want to be. Because then you can just be like, ‘Ugh, those dickheads.’”

Brain Computes Motion Direction Independently From the Eye

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Researchers have developed a high-throughput brain-mapping tool that reveals the brain does not simply rely on pre-calculated motion signals from the eye, but also computes the direction of movement independently. The study mapped nearly 46,000 mouse brain cells to uncover a dual visual system in the superior colliculus, separating localized object tracking from global self-motion sensing.

Whether ducking a fast-moving ball, stepping back from an oncoming vehicle, or evading an approaching predator, survival depends on the brain’s ability to determine not just that an object is moving, but its exact trajectory in real time.

For decades, visual neuroscientists knew that specialized neurons in the mammalian retina fire selectively when stimuli move in specific directions, left, right, up, down, or diagonally.

Because these retinal ganglion cells already encode directional vectors, researchers widely assumed the brain simply inherited that pre-calculated motion data passively from the eye. However, no mapping technology existed that could simultaneously trace exact synaptic connections from the retina while identifying the molecular identity of every receiving brain cell.

Now, in a study published in Nature, researchers at UC San Francisco (UCSF) have bridged that technological gap.

Led by co-senior authors Dr. Xin Duan, professor of ophthalmology, and Dr. Evan Feinberg, professor of anatomy, alongside postdoctoral fellow Dr. Fei Wang, the team built a scalable eye-to-brain wiring atlas revealing that specialized circuits in the midbrain compute motion direction completely on their own.

Over the past decade, Duan’s laboratory pioneered anterograde trans-neuronal tracing, a technique in which a fluorescent molecular tracer delivered to the eye crosses synaptic junctions to illuminate only those downstream brain neurons directly receiving retinal input.

To scale this tool from single-circuit tracing into a comprehensive atlas, Wang paired the trans-neuronal tracer with MERFISH (multiplexed error-robust fluorescence in situ hybridization), a spatial transcriptomic method that reads gene activity across thousands of individual cells in a tissue slice simultaneously to pinpoint each neuron’s exact genetic identity.

Applying this combined platform across nearly 46,000 cells in the mouse visual system, the investigators uncovered hundreds of distinct retinal-to-brain connection pairs.

“It’s like an entire atlas we’ve built,” Wang said. “We’ve uncovered hundreds of pairs of connections between the eye and the brain. We ended up focusing on only one page of this atlas, but there are many left to study in the future.”

Focusing on the superior colliculus, an evolutionarily conserved midbrain hub essential for visual orientation and rapid motor reflexes, Wang, Feinberg, and Duan recorded real-time neural firing while showing mice high-contrast black bars scrolling across a screen in eight distinct directions.

Combining functional recordings with their molecular wiring map exposed two separate motion-processing architectures operating side by side:

“We think these sets of cells are doing slightly different things from each other,” Wang explained. “The eye cells detecting direction may specialize in small, local motions like prey or food. The brain cells computing direction sense a larger background movement.”

The investigators hypothesize that this global background motion signal informs an organism of its own self-motion and spatial trajectory as it navigates through the environment.

Beyond resolving a foundational question in visual neuroscience, the high-resolution wiring atlas carries direct translational implications for regenerative medicine and neuroengineering. As researchers advance toward optic nerve regeneration, whole-eye transplantation, and high-resolution visual brain-machine interfaces for patients with glaucoma, trauma, or multiple sclerosis, restoring functional sight requires reconnecting damaged axons to their exact functional targets.

“This is a fundamental mechanism that has been understudied from a basic science perspective, but it also has applied value,” said Duan. “If we ever want to repair or restore vision in people, we need to know which neural connections need to be rewired for functional vision.”

“In addition to providing a blueprint for revealing structural maps of the brain, our study illustrates how the resulting maps can be used to answer longstanding questions regarding brain function,” added Feinberg.

The team notes that their scalable tracer-plus-MERFISH platform can be adapted to map how the eye regulates circadian rhythms, and generalized to chart cell-to-cell connectivity across any organ system in the body.

Does the eye do all the work of detecting which way things move?

No. While specialized cells in the retina detect motion direction and send that data to the brain, UCSF researchers discovered a second group of neurons in the brain’s superior colliculus that independently calculates motion direction using basic visual inputs, even when the eye’s own direction-sensing cells are turned off.

Why does the brain have two different systems for sensing motion?

Researchers believe the two pathways handle different visual jobs: the eye’s direction-sensing cells appear specialized for tracking small, local moving targets (like food, prey, or a flying ball), while the brain’s independent motion-computing cells detect large-scale background movement to help track how your own body is moving through space.

How could this brain-mapping tool help restore vision in the future?

To successfully develop eye transplants, optic nerve repairs, or visual brain-machine interfaces for conditions like glaucoma or multiple sclerosis, scientists need an exact wiring diagram showing which eye cells connect to which functional brain circuits. This new atlas provides that precise rewiring blueprint.

TransA-MERFISH reveals divergent post-retinal direction-selective circuits

A major goal in neuroscience is to map the transformation of information as it flows through the brain. For example, neurons at many levels of the visual hierarchy are attuned to motion direction. However, links between motion-selective computations and cell types at various stages of the visual hierarchy remain unidentified. A key first step in delineating these relationships is to map the wiring diagrams that connect different motion-selective cell types.

Here we establish a systematic, cell-type-specific circuit-mapping method, combining an anterograde trans-neuronal fluorescent tracer (mWGA–mCherry) with spatial transcriptomics (multiplexed error-robust fluorescence in situ hybridization (MERFISH)) to establish TransA-MERFISH. We used TransA-MERFISH to comprehensively map the synaptic target cell types of four retinal ganglion cell subclasses in superior colliculus (SC) at cell-type resolution.

These experiments unexpectedly revealed that retinal on–off direction-selective ganglion cells (ooDSGCs) innervate several direction-selective SC cell types, but do not innervate a SC cell type that was previously reported to display strong direction-selective responses.

We validated these results using orthogonal approaches and demonstrated the sensitivity and specificity of TransA-MERFISH. Functional studies of SC activity before and after ablation of retinal direction-selective circuits confirmed that these computationally distinct, ooDSGC-dependent and -independent direction-selective cell types are commingled in SC.

Together, our study establishes TransA-MERFISH as a robust framework for mapping wiring diagrams at the cell-type level and uncovers divergent computational mechanisms of motion detection in early visual processing.

Sky Sports: Man Utd Plotting £67m Move for Newcastle Star Malick Thiaw

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are looking to bolster their defensive ranks in 2027, and a new target has emerged as a possibility for Mauricio Pochettino.

The Argentine has replaced Michael Carrick as the boss at Old Trafford this week, and will lead the team into a new era, with a defensive rebuild planned for the new year in both January and the summer.

Harry Maguire, Luke Shaw, and Lisandro Martinez are all out of contract at the end of the current campaign, and it seems unlikely that any of them will be staying at the club beyond this season. New arrivals are expected to take their place, and the club are once again looking at Premier League players who can come in to make an instant impact.

A number of central defenders have been linked with a January move to the club, including Brentford captain Nathan Collins and Fulham star Calvin Bassey.

But it seems United are in contact with a number of players at the Unique Sports Group agency, who represent Bassey, as a report by Sky Sports Germany states that the club are also showing a strong interest in Newcastle United star Malick Thiaw.

The 25-year-old joined the Magpies in the summer of 2025 from AC Milan in a deal worth around £30m, and immediately established himself as a key player. His status in the squad was cemented this summer, when he was appointed into the club's leadership group, and he has worn the captain's armband on a number of occasions this season.

Thiaw is now valued at £67m by Newcastle, but it's reported that a summer exit in 2027 has not been ruled out, with Man Utd among the most interested parties for his signature.

Regarded as the 'undisputed leader' of the defence at St James' Park, the club do not want to lose him, but accept that they may be forced to sell for a big fee if Champions League clubs come calling, and they are unable to turn their slow start to the season around.

The 6ft 4 star has also recently broken into the Germany first-team, with Jurgen Klopp understood to be a huge fan of his qualities. His current contract is due to expire in 2029.

Man Utd need defensive reinforcements, and so it's no surprise that a number of players are going to start being linked with a move to Old Trafford as a result.

INEOS have shown that they prefer to shop in the Premier League since taking over, and Thiaw would fit that. He is also just 25 and has his prime years ahead of him, while he has the experience of playing for a big club like AC Milan in his career too.

But with Newcastle likely to demand a huge fee to let him leave, the Red Devils could surely find a player of comparable quality for a much lesser fee, and free up funds to make other signings instead.

Thiaw has his qualities, but we have seen him exposed in the Premier League before. Until there is a leader and proven starter in the backline to help guide him along, United would be making a huge gamble by spending such big money on bringing him in at this stage of their rebuild under Pochettino.

United simply cannot afford to get their signings wrong at this stage, and with Newcastle certain to play hardball for any deal, they'd be better off looking elsewhere unless they are 100% convinced he is the right man for the role and the new manager.

And it’s only been two years since Dickinson released his last solo album, The Mandrake Project.

Dickinson sings the PsychoGod song “Speaking in Tongues” with his trademark urgency. Its intro recalls Maiden’s “Fear of the Dark,” but it soon becomes a unique plea for understanding all leading to the revelation: “Tell the truth, and the world will know just what you mean.”

“‘Speaking in tongues’ is an expression used to describe a form of religious or sacred ecstasy in which celebrants often lose control of normal speech and bodily functions,” Dickinson said in a statement. “Their search for truth at the expense of sanity contrasts sharply with the shallow world of the Internet, social media and the narcissist political classes that seem to dominate our lives.”

The video, which Dickinson co-directed with Leo Liberti, shows the singer both shouting at the heavens in a Nevada desert as his band plays and under arrest in orange prison gear as he’s accused of trafficking drugs in L.A. Dickinson said in a statement that the video is the first of five interconnected stories, “a karmic journey” connected by the album’s songs. “As a first video, ‘Speaking in Tongues’ is brutal, violent and [it] holds a mirror to the ugly global world of accusation, incarceration, conspiracy, and lies. Free daily briefing on global business news.

Skydance Co-CEO David Ellison confirmed Wednesday that Elon Musk is a shareholder in the company and said Skydance would welcome a future collaboration with the Tesla $TSLA and SpaceX CEO, according to Bloomberg.

Ellison spoke at the New York Times DealBook Summit in New York. He called Musk "the greatest entrepreneur of our time," according to Bloomberg. "To have his vote of confidence and to have him as a shareholder is an honor and a privilege," Ellison said.

“If there was any opportunity to be able to collaborate with him in the future ... that is the appropriate opportunity in the future, we’d be thrilled to do so,” Ellison said. He added that no collaborations are under way.

Earlier reports put Musk's investment at about $1 billion, according to Bloomberg. Ellison would not say if the stake is a first step toward a broader tie-up involving Musk's Starlink, according to Deadline. "There is nothing we can comment on," Ellison said, according to Deadline.

Skydance’s HBO Max will carry “Musk,” a documentary that takes a critical view of the tech founder, when it debuts in the first quarter of next year, according to Bloomberg. Asked about Alex Gibney's film, Ellison said Skydance has a deal with A24, the producer, and will honor it, according to Deadline. "We believe in independence. We don't believe in censorship of any kind," Ellison said.

John Malone, chairman emeritus of Liberty Media, predicted last month that SpaceX would invest $8 billion to $10 billion in Skydance and that the two companies would reach a global output deal. Malone cited Musk's relationship with Ellison's father, Oracle $ORCL co-founder Larry Ellison, who helped finance Musk's purchase of Twitter $TWTR.

Skydance completed its all-cash acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery on Oct. 6. The combined company trades on the New York Stock Exchange under the ticker SKYD.

Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller wants the central bank to get more aggressive with interest rate hikes, arguing that stubborn inflation may require a 50-basis-point increase in December rather than the smaller move investors expect.

Speaking during a CNBC interview Wednesday, Waller said the Fed must either deliver a half-percentage-point increase at its December 8-9 meeting or explicitly signal that another hike will follow in January.

“You might need to, like, shock people,” Waller said, referring to the central bank's efforts to prevent businesses and consumers from accepting inflation near 3% as normal.

“My view is then you have to go 50 in December to put it in place and get it done,” he said, warning that insufficiently aggressive action could weaken expectations that inflation will return to the Fed's target.

Waller's concerns follow three consecutive months in which core consumer prices rose 0.3%, a pace equivalent to roughly 3.5% inflation annually. That's considerably higher than the Fed's 2% target, which is formally measured using a separate inflation indicator known as the Personal Consumption Expenditures price index.

The governor had already established his threshold for a more aggressive move in a November 10 speech. He said another 0.3% core CPI reading would warrant considering a half-point hike, arguing that moving only 25 basis points could damage the Fed's credibility.

October inflation subsequently met that condition. Headline consumer prices rose 0.4% during the month, while core inflation climbed 0.3%, both exceeding economists' forecasts.

Waller's latest comments suggest he is increasingly concerned about the psychology of inflation, particularly the risk that companies begin treating annual price increases of 3% as acceptable and routinely pass higher costs to customers.

The proposed increase would lift the Fed's benchmark rate from its current range of 3.75%-4% to 4.25%-4.50%. Two consecutive quarter-point hikes in December and January would reach the same destination, but Waller argues that timing and communication matter when inflation expectations are at risk.

Markets remain less convinced that the Fed will move that aggressively in December. Futures pricing Wednesday morning showed roughly a 95% probability of a 25-basis-point increase and only about a 5% probability of a half-point move

The prospect of higher borrowing costs has been particularly painful for crypto investors. Higher interest rates make Treasury securities and other interest-bearing investments more competitive with speculative assets, while tighter financial conditions can reduce the money available for riskier bets.

Bitcoin has already suffered a sharp selloff amid the changing rate outlook. As Decrypt previously covered, persistent inflation fears have coincided with withdrawals from spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds and weakening technical indicators, adding pressure to an already fragile market.

Waller's increasingly hawkish monetary-policy stance comes alongside a more accommodating view of blockchain networks, although he sees established financial infrastructure as essential to its adoption.

In a separate November 17 speech at The Clearing House Annual Conference in New York, Waller discussed how tokenization—the representation of financial assets on digital networks—could reshape payments and securities markets. That speech focused on financial technology rather than interest-rate policy.

He pointed to a growing convergence between traditional markets and blockchain platforms, with banks and payment providers experimenting with tokenized deposits, stablecoins, and programmable transactions.

Waller described how tokenized money could allow investors to purchase securities outside conventional trading hours, with payments and assets changing hands simultaneously through smart contracts.

But he rejected the idea that the development of blockchain-based markets necessarily makes existing payment infrastructure obsolete.

“Central bank money need not be used in every transaction or on every platform, but its presence at the foundation of the system is essential,” Waller said.
